#6954. Survey: Organizations Report Positive ROI on AI Investments, Yet Data Readiness and Oversight Lag Behind

According to a survey by data and AI services provider Further, enterprises are reporting benefits from artificial intelligence even as their data preparation and oversight processes remain incomplete.

The company's recent State of Enterprise AI Maturity research surveyed 515 U.S.-based AI decision-makers. Just 5% believed their organizations' data was largely ready for AI.

The findings suggest that gaining value from individual AI applications and preparing to operate them more broadly are separate challenges. Reported returns alone do not establish that an organization has the foundations to sustain or expand those results.

Further found that 37% of respondents' organizations had paused, scaled back, or abandoned an underperforming AI initiative in the previous 12 months. Among that group, data quality or availability was the most frequently cited main reason, at 24%.

Meanwhile, 29% reported completing a formal data audit and remediation plan. The research also placed 69% of organizations in the exploring, experimenting, or implementing stages of adoption.

Further distinguishes data prepared for conventional reporting from data prepared for AI. Its explanation emphasizes data origins and documentation, along with access controls that support ongoing evaluation and governance.

For readers assessing their own readiness, the practical issue is whether data supports the requirements of a particular application. A collection suitable for a dashboard may need additional preparation before it can reliably support an AI system.

Seventy-seven percent reported a positive return on AI investment. However, only 3% said their organizations had a documented AI-audit process.

The findings measure different things: perceived financial benefit and the existence of a repeatable oversight process. Neither substitutes for the other.

"In reality, confidence without a documented process is exposure," said Kierra Dotson, associate director of AI strategy and governance at Further. "Closing that gap doesn't take new technology; it requires an auditable governance process."

For the full report, visit the Further site (registration required).

#6952. 11 IKEA Kitchen Finds Designers Swear By in Their Own Homes

These finds deliver big style without breaking the budget.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Interior designers always know where to source the “good stuff,” and over the years we’ve gotten them to divulge their favorite secret spots for furniture and decor. The selections run the gamut from luxurious to affordable, showing that as long as you have an eye for design, even a budget-friendly pick can feel like a million bucks.

One affordable spot all designers keep coming back to is, unsurprisingly, IKEA. You can find anything you need at the Swedish retailer, often at unbeatable prices. We’ve already spoken with designers about what they generally like to buy from the brand, so this time around we’re getting more granular: we asked several pros what they like to buy for their kitchen, specifically.

Why this space? Well, our team is personally obsessed with this area of the home, and if you didn’t know, IKEA has an expansive array of kitchen products. From glassware and tableware to built-ins that make your cookspace look custom, IKEA has just about everything you need for your kitchen. Whether you’re looking for shopping inspiration or want to pick up designer-approved picks, check out what our favorite experts are buying from IKEA ahead.

Since most people have kitchen rugs, why not choose something stylish? I prefer flatwoven rugs that have texture and color variations in kitchen spaces so that they can be easily cleaned and can hide small spills or crumbs.” —Vicki Taylor of Tailored Spaces

#6951. Don’t Ask Trump How His Revenge Tour Is Going

The president keeps trying and failing to legally ruin his enemies.

“Take 276 / You know this used to be fun.”

The problem is that there was no ham in the ham sandwich.

#6949. Researchers Find AI Is Accelerating Vulnerability Discovery

AI-powered vulnerability research is contributing to a major increase in security updates, raising concerns that IT teams could fall behind as patching demands grow, according to cybersecurity company ThreatDown.

In a recent blog post, the company stated that in September 2026, vendors addressed 998 common vulnerabilities and exposures (CVEs) around Patch Tuesday, compared with 96 during the same period last year. In addition, the three-month rolling average of patched Linux kernel vulnerabilities tripled year over year, while Google's Chrome browser experienced a 36-fold increase.

The researchers point to advanced AI models, including Anthropic's Claude Mythos Preview, as a major factor behind the increase in vulnerability discovery.

Introduced in April 2026, Mythos Preview is being used through Project Glasswing, an initiative involving Microsoft, the Linux Foundation and other organizations to identify vulnerabilities in critical software.

While AI could ultimately make software more secure, the cybersecurity company warns that vulnerability discovery is accelerating faster than organizations can deploy fixes.

IT teams must still evaluate patches, test compatibility and schedule updates without disrupting critical business operations.

The challenge is particularly significant for organizations already struggling with patch management.

The cybersecurity vendor cites Verizon's 2026 Data Breach Investigations Report, which found that 35% of known actively exploited vulnerabilities remained unpatched after 28 days in 2025.

Ryan Powell, vice president of IT and security at ThreatDown, said automated patching systems can help organizations manage the growing workload. However, additional patches still require more testing and validation, increasing operational overhead and the risks associated with frequent system changes.

The report also highlights the risks of vulnerabilities in widely used software components such as OpenSSL, embedded in applications, network devices, and security products.

Unlike standalone applications, these components often depend on downstream vendors to release updates before organizations can deploy fixes.

The security firm's analysis points to CVE-2022-0778, an OpenSSL vulnerability patched in March 2022, as an example. Some affected products did not receive updates until several months later.

As AI accelerates vulnerability discovery, these dependencies could delay remediation and extend organizations' exposure to known security flaws.

ThreatDown's findings illustrate a growing challenge for enterprise cybersecurity: AI is making vulnerabilities easier to discover but fixing them remains a complex and time-consuming process.

For the full report, visit the ThreatDown blog here.

Class CrunchLabs, the suite of free curriculum resources from NASA engineer turned YouTube science communicator Mark Rober, has released its first two courses on YouTube.

Leaders in the artificial intelligence industry have warned for years that increasingly capable AI systems could eventually become dangerous, all the while continuing to race to build them. Now, their sentiments have changed.

Increasingly, cyber criminals are sharing AI prompts, jailbreak techniques, and access to existing models that can be incorporated into fraud and phishing operations, according the latest AI Threat Report from Flashpoint.

Cloud Security Alliance's 2026 Top Threats report ranks identity as the leading cloud security concern, followed by two new AI-related risks.

#6948. Rosie Huntington-Whiteley on the Quiet Power of Saint Laurent

The latest Women’s Ready-to-Wear collection and the house’s new Simone bag speak to a style built on restraint, tension, and silent confidence.

Rosie Huntington-Whiteley has one rule when she gets dressed: “I never want to feel as though the clothes are wearing me.” It has served her well for more than two decades, first as one of fashion’s most recognizable faces, then as muse, and now now as an entrepreneur in her own right. It also embodies why Saint Laurent has been a constant.

“I have always felt like a Saint Laurent woman,” she says. Part of the pull is the house’s history, a way of dressing that Yves Saint Laurent defined in the 1960s that still feels charged today. Part of it is simpler: she has always wanted clothes that let her feel like herself, and Saint Laurent does that artfully.

For Fall 26, the collection harkens the house’s founding codes with tailoring that feels looser and more sensual than ever. It was the first thing Huntington-Whiteley noticed. “There’s something incredibly powerful about that Saint Laurent silhouette, particularly the way it feels so fluid and sensual this season,” she reflects. “I’ve always loved a beautifully cut suit worn against bare skin.”

The season’s other headline is Simone, a new bag drawn from Monsieur Saint Laurent’s personal letters. The envelope pouches he favored in the 1970s and 1980s were made for evening. This iteration of the shape is made for every day, with a softer, more structured build, two compartments, a zipped pocket inside, and a magnetic snap that keeps the line clean. On the front sits the new double Cassandre, a raised, dimensional take on the house monogram.

It suits the way Huntington-Whiteley thinks about her own closet. Her most treasured pieces are the Saint Laurent leather jackets she has collected over the years. “There’s something about a Saint Laurent leather jacket that only gets better with time,” she says. “Each one begins to hold memories and becomes part of your identity.” Simone is made in that same spirit. Cut in supple leather and meant to be carried every day rather than saved for a night out, it is a bag designed to be lived in, and to collect a few memories of its own.

Huntington-Whiteley’s own style comes is built on constants. “I’m drawn to a sense of restraint, clean lines, strong silhouettes, beautiful materials and pieces that have a timeless quality to them,” she offers. “There’s often a tension between the masculine and feminine in the way I dress.”

That push and pull is what keeps her coming back to Saint Laurent. “Masculine and feminine, restraint and sensuality, strength and vulnerability,” she says. “Those contradictions have always been at the heart of Saint Laurent.” More than six decades after Yves Saint Laurent founded his house in Paris, it is still finding new ways to make those codes feel modern.

Ask her to sum up the Saint Laurent woman and she doesn’t hesitate. “She’s sensual without being overtly feminine, powerful without losing her sexuality, and there’s always an element of mystery,” she says. “It’s an aesthetic that doesn’t ask for attention, but inevitably commands it.”

Saint Laurent by Anthony Vaccarello single-breasted jacket in herringbone wool, laced camisole in silk satin, culottes in herringbone wool, Oval Buckle belt in smooth leather and Voltaire slingback pumps in glazed leather.

Saint Laurent by Anthony Vaccarello lace bodysuit.

Saint Laurent by Anthony Vaccarello cocoon coat in lacquered canvas and shearling.

#6947. 15 Paint Colors HGTV Stars Swear By for a Home That Looks Expensive

From sea blue to crisp white, these designer favorites are timeless.

Everyone knows that a quick coat of paint is the fastest way to transform a room. It’s a favorite trick among professional designers and DIYers alike, but choosing the perfect shade isn't always easy. With hundreds of colors to choose from, finding “the one” can quickly become overwhelming. If you’re feeling a little stuck, don’t fret: HGTV’s biggest stars make these tough decisions every day.

Whether they’re renovating an outdated home or putting the finishing touches on a dream build, HGTV personalities are constantly selecting paint colors that make a space feel fresh, spacious, inviting, and beautifully designed. Over the years, they’ve crowned a select few as go-to favorites that are foolproof and versatile enough to work in almost any room. When every renovation comes with deadlines and budgets, dependable paint colors become invaluable.

To help narrow your search, we asked some of our favorite HGTV stars to reveal the paint colors they reach for most often. Their picks include everything from versatile neutrals to powdery pastels that work across a variety of homes and aesthetics. Additional copy by Alyssa Khan.

“We love muted colors because they’re playful, but not over-the-top. Behr Mocha Ice is a perfect example of that balance—it brings the personality you need to take a space from basic and bland to warm and interesting. Try it in a bedroom, dining room, or anywhere that’s missing that ‘wow’ factor.”—Drew Scott, Property Brothers

“This beautiful blue is timeless and versatile. It’s calming, but not boring. Even though Behr's Dragonfly makes a statement, it’s still neutral enough for many spaces, whether it’s on every wall in a bedroom or used on a specific feature like kitchen cabinets.”—Jonathan Scott, Property Brothers

“This never-boring black paint color is dramatic and impactful yet very versatile. Pair with white for a timeless aesthetic. Use it to create a dramatic accent wall or outside for your exterior (like we did) or trim for maximum curb appeal.”—Tarek El Moussa, The Flip Off

“My favorite paint color is Tricorn Black by Sherwin-Williams. It’s bold, clean, and makes every detail pop. Whether it’s a door, a wall, or literally anything else, this black brings that high-end look every single time. Never fails.”—Cole DeBoer, Down Home Fab

“I am currently loving greige, the perfect blend of gray and beige. It’s warm, soft, and timeless—a great option for homeowners who want both cozy and modern. Benjamin Moore's Revere Pewter is a classic and rich greige with warmth. Great for open-concept homes.”—Heather Rae El Moussa, The Flip Off

“What I love most about it is its confidence. Studio Green by Farrow & Ball doesn’t whisper: It commands. It’s deep, moody, and unapologetically rich, the kind of color that instantly elevates a space and gives it a point of view. In daylight, it reads as a classic, almost architectural green, grounded and refined. But at night? It transforms into something velvety and dramatic, wrapping the room in a quiet luxury that feels both intimate and powerful.”—Egypt Sherrod, Married to Real Estate andProperty Virgins

“Benjamin Moore Misty Air OC-44 is my constant when I need a bright neutral. It is a broken-in cream that’s the perfect backdrop for colorful art and upholstery.”—Erin Napier, Home Town

““My current favorite paint color is Black Fox by Sherwin-Williams. It’s the perfect mix of sophisticated and moody, with just enough warmth to make any space feel grounded and timeless. It pairs beautifully with so many other tones and textures. I love it in an office!”—Chelsea DeBoer, Down Home Fab

Magnolia Home by Joanna Gaines Juniper Tree Crafted by KILZ

“Seed & Supply was one of the first additions we made to the Silos back in 2015. Before it served as our garden shop on the grounds, it was used as an additional retail space at the original Magnolia shop, which we lovingly refer to as The Little Shop on Bosque. This year, the team had the idea to evolve the building yet again, but this time to serve as a creative workshop space for our guests. I immediately loved the idea. It has always been my hope that our guests’ experience at the Silos would inspire creativity and connection.

The whole interior of the workshop is refreshed with one color, Juniper Tree Trim, Door, and Cabinetry Paint in satin finish. I love how these nature-forward hues turned out—they feel fresh yet familiar, bold yet comforting.”—Joanna Gaines, Fixer Upper

““White Flour is the perfect soft white backdrop for every style. It accentuates simplicity or maximalist energy. The warm hue is anything but basic.”—Taniya Nayak, Battle on the Beach

“I love Farrow & Ball's Drop Cloth for walls, or for trim and cabinetry with a more true white on the wall. It is a timeless neutral that looks beautiful, particularly in historic homes. This warm neutral evokes calm tranquility without being sterile. It is the perfect first layer for any space: It sets a foundation of simplicity that can easily be layered upon with color on cabinetry, trim detail, furniture, art, and décor.”—Jenny Marrs, Fixer to Fabulous

“I keep going back to Behr White. It’s porcelain-like and a classic white without being too stark or jarring. It also has a very slight undertone of blue, which I prefer over yellow or pink undertones, as a slight hint of blue preserves more of a pure white tone.”—Galey Alix, Home in a Heartbeat With Galey Alix

“My current favorite color is Benjamin Moore Windsor Green. I just painted my bedroom this color! The walls, the doors, the windows, and the trim. It's just so deep and lovely. To me, a deep mossy green is cozy and sophisticated at the same time. It steals the show every time, which is why I went for it in my own bedroom!”—Kristina Crestin, Farmhouse Fixer

In her blog for the Laurel Mercantile, the Home Town Takeover co-host crowned Dover White her all-time favorite hue.

“My all-time favorite white, forever and always, to the point my design team just defaults to it when they aren't able to get a quick decision from me.”— Erin Napier, Home Town Takeover

Fix My Flip star, Page Turner, shared her favorite shade of white with HGTV fans. Boasting warm cream and soft beige undertones, Whisper White is the perfect airy neutral.

“I like how this shade isn’t as stark as other white paints can be.” — Page Turner, Fix My Flip

The Property Brothers named Grenada Villa as one of their go-to shades for their site, drew & jonathan, but fans of Brother vs. Brother will recall the shade being name-dropped much earlier. Drew used it to coat the guest bedroom ceiling in the Season 6 Canyon House in 2018. The serene shade has remained in their palette ever since.
